For example, specific ores, rare earths, etc.\nPriority -1: Type filter routes. These are your \"default\" paths that are taken if nothing overrides them.\nPriority -2: Output routes. This goes to the final output chest.\nThe simplest version of this is an input chest set to extract always, some conduits inbetween, and an output chest at -2 priority. Then everything you input (ores, random tinker tools by accident, w/e) will go to the output chest. But obviously this isn't very useful.\nSo you start adding \" modules \" to your inbetween conduits. These provide routes for ores to go. Let's start with a macerator module."
- candidate chunk-06d40bd7fed30e57f17a1a25576898d6 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > EV Applied Energistics 2] text="EV Applied Energistics 2\nApplied Energistics (henceforth just referred to as AE) is the most commonly used logistics system for mid-late game. As such, it finds common use in high-tier ore-processing solutions designed by players. From EV - ZPM, the multiblock miners ( Ore Drilling Plants I/II/III/IV)(ODPs) become available. At IV and beyond (up to a point), single-block machines are no longer acceptable for the amount of ores we will be processing, and instead we will move on to the GT++ multiblock machines. These are:\n- Ore Washing Plant (OWP)\n- Maceration Stack (MS)\n- Large Thermal Refinery (LTR). This is the multiblock thermal centrifuge.\n- Industrial Centrifuge (IC)\n- Large Sifter (LS).\n- Industrial Electrolyzer (IE)\n- Utupu-Tanuri / Dehydrator (only necessary for Rare Earths and a few Toxic Everglades ores)\nOther multiblock machines that we will be using are the Large Chemical Reactors (LCRs).\nFor a typical ore processing setup at EV - ZPM, we generally have the following desires.\n- Maximise returns. If we throw in an ore that has tertiary byproducts, we want all the byproducts. This requires use of a thermal centrifuge. Thus, our system will make use of thermal centrifuges, especially for gallium\n- Maximise throughput. ODPs are capable of immense output, and an inadequate setup will choke and die. In addition, Void Miners (VMs) become available near the tail end of this period, and these will overwhelm unprepared systems, especially when boosted with the noble gases.\n- Indium retrieval. Indium is a particular metal needed for specific circuit components (namely as indium gallium phosphide, or InGaP). There are various ways to get this metal. Replication with UU-Matter (slow and painful), fusion from lithium and silver (requires T3 Fusion , unlocked in UV ), milling sphalerite (unlocked in LuV ), processing Naquadria (unlocked in LuV ), processing Roquesite or pure Indium ore (extremely rare) or extraction from purified Sphalerite and Galena ores .\n- Lanthanide processing. This processing line takes in crushed Bastnasite or crushed Monazite to yield the different lanthanide elements.\nFor our purposes, we will only need 6 specific AE components in order to create a rudimentary, and yet infinitely expandable AE ore processing system:\n- ME Controller. This is basic and fundamental. You will need at least 1 to be the brains of your ore processing subnet. Each exposed face can connect to and control up to 32 other AE devices,"
- candidate chunk-65ae19f4f0875a16975b71c9f7385bb0 doc=doc-45ac5ed5cca700a24048b98b03470923 sections=[Planets > Tier 8] text="Tier 8\nSurface of T Ceti E\nT8 T Ceti E\nOre Vein | Weight | Frequency | Small Ore | Per Chunk | Fluid Reservoir | Size\nMagnetite & Gold | 160 | 17.6% | Neutronium Ore | 8 | Distilled Water | 0-10000\nMagnetite | 160 | 17.6% | Lapis Ore | 4 | Hydrogen Gas | 0-700\nMagnesite | 110 | 12.1% | Aw. Draconium Ore | 2 | Very Heavy Oil | 0-200\nBauxite | 80 | 8.8%\nApatite | 60 | 6.6%\nKaolinite | 60 | 6.6%\nGarnet Sand & Nether Star | 60 | 6.6%\nTin & Cassiterite | 50 | 5.5%\nRock Salt & Salt | 50 | 5.5%\nBlack Plutonium | 40 | 4.4%\nOilsands | 40 | 4.4%\nQuartzite & Quartz | 20 | 2.2%\nIlmenite | 16 | 1.8%\nChrome | 5 | 0.5%\nOther Notes\nSeaweed needed for Xenoxene covers the floors of the oceans. Dimension required to breed the Seaweed bee.\nT8 Barnard C\nOre Vein | Weight | Frequency | Small Ore | Per Chunk | Fluid Reservoir | Size\nLignite Coal | 160 | 32.9% | Very Heavy Oil | 0-800\nBasaltic Mineral Sand | 80 | 16.5% | Unknown Liquid | 0-300\nArsenic | 60 | 12.3%\nRock Salt & Salt | 50 | 10.3%\nBlack Plutonium | 40 | 8.2%\nOilsands | 40 | 8.2%\nKyanite & Mica | 20 | 4.1%\nTungsten | 16 | 3.3%\nNickel & Osmium | 10 | 2.1%\nSamarium | 10 | 2.1%\nOther Notes\nLogs needed for Radox spawn as trees on the surface. Lakes of Unknown Liquid spawn randomly on the surface. Rainbow Crystals spawn randomly all over the world.\nT8 Barnard E\nOre Vein | Weight | Frequency | Small Ore | Per Chunk | Fluid Reservoir | Size\nMagnetite | 160 | 27.1% | Titanium Ore | 32 | Liquid Air | 0-400\nChalcopyrite & Copper | 80 | 13.5% | Zinc Ore | 24\nGarnet Sand & Nether Star | 60 | 10.2% | Iron Ore | 16\nBentonite & Olivine | 60 | 10.2% | Naquadah Ore | 8\nThorium | 60 | 10.2% | Neutronium Ore | 8\nCadmium & Rare Earth | 40 | 6.8% | Nickel Ore | 8\nGold & Infused Gold | 30 | 5.1% | Black Plutonium Ore | 6\nNaquadah | 30 | 5.1% |"
- candidate chunk-eba529c2606e0f9cb9b5b4980bcfc289 doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV) > Side Objectives > The End and pre-Moon Titanium] text="The End and pre-Moon Titanium\nDiagram showing processing steps for making Titanium Ingots from Naquadah Ore. EBF - Electric Blast Furnace; LCR - Large Chemical Reactor; DT - Distillation Tower.\nEyes of Ender can be made in an HV Chemical Reactor and used to find and activate the End Portal. After defeating the Ender Dragon, the player can travel to the Far End Asteroids where they will occasionally find veins of Naquadah Ore. This allows the player to produce Titanium Ingots without traveling to the Moon.\nRaw Naquadah Ore goes through the usual ore processing and becomes Naquadah Oxide Mixture Dust. Two dust can be processed in the Electric Blast Furnace with 3000L of Fluoroantimonic Acid to produce 4 Titanium Trifluoride Dust, which is then put back into the EBF, which must be overclocked to EV , with 3000L of Hydrogen to get a Hot Titanium Ingot. Cool the ingot in a Vacuum Freezer to obtain a Titanium Ingot.\nThe production of Fluoroantimonic Acid is an involved process which requires a Large Chemical Reactor that is overclocked to EV. A Distillation Tower is not necessary for the production of Antimony Pentachloride but will refund all of the Ether used in the production of Antimony Trichloride Solution.\nThe diagram to the right shows the processing steps for obtaining Titanium Ingots from Naquadah Ore. Note that Ethylene and Hydrogen Sulfide are considered inputs, rather than being part of the production chain, as they can be obtained in great quantities from processing Oil and its byproducts."
- candidate chunk-899f089c10d9b05623dd29d55ffafa96 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > EV Applied Energistics 2] text="Lanthanide processing. This processing line takes in crushed Bastnasite or crushed Monazite to yield the different lanthanide elements.\nFor our purposes, we will only need 6 specific AE components in order to create a rudimentary, and yet infinitely expandable AE ore processing system:\n- ME Controller. This is basic and fundamental. You will need at least 1 to be the brains of your ore processing subnet. Each exposed face can connect to and control up to 32 other AE devices, giving our 1-block controller control over a maximum of 192 devices.\n- ME Drive. This will serve as an overflow buffer, just in case all your machines suffer maintenance issues and shutdown simultaneously.\n- ME Storage Cell. Place inside the ME Drive. You will need at least 1.\n- ME Interfaces (the full block type). These will serve as the import blocks, from which the outputs of your machines will be pushed into to enter the AE network. How many you need depends on how creative you are with your GT piping.\n- ME Storage Buses. You will need 1 for each input bus of your multiblock machines, and one extra to handle overflow (optional).\n- ME OreDictionary Filter Cards. This is the magic that makes AE amazing for ore processing. You will need at least 1 per storage bus, with some exceptions.\nNow, let's talk more about the ME OreDictionary Filter Card, or OreDict cards for short. Many GT items have an OreDictionary Name, that looks something like (item type)(name) . There are OreDict names for the ore block, each of the intermediates during the ore processing steps, and the final pure dust. Using galena as an example, they are:\n- Galena Ore. oreGalena\n- Raw Galena Ore. rawOreGalena\n- Crushed Galena Ore. crushedGalena\n- Purified Galena Ore. crushedPurifiedGalena\n- Centrifuged Galena Ore. crushedCentrifugedGalena\n- Impure Pile of Galena Dust dustImpureGalena\n- Purified Pile of Galena Dust dustPureGalena\n- Galena Dust dustGalena\nME Storage Buses attempt to push out compatible items, when immediately received by the network via either ME Interfaces or ME Import Buses, into the inventories that they face. Conventionally, these would be storage blocks like chests/drawers/barrels. But note that Input Buses also have internal inventories. Thus, when we place an OreDict card inside a ME Storage Bus facing towards an Input Bus, we have created an intelligent exporter of items!\nAnother thing to note is that"
- candidate chunk-c9c1517071de4cd1d65e85ed12a394c8 doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV) > Progression > Side quest 2: Multiblock ore processing] text="Side quest 2: Multiblock ore processing\nAs you entered EV, you gained access to multiblock counterparts of centrifuge, thermal centrifuge, ore washer/chemical bath, and crusher. They are way more efficient compared to their singleblock variants, however they require large amounts of resources like Titanium and most are gated behind TPV coils, so they're listed here instead of in early EV. If your main resource income is from bees, centrifuge is recommended over all other multiblocks as it speeds up centrifuging combs significantly."
- candidate chunk-7204ffb05b8f1f010ede14e35607675b doc=doc-0acf46d9067c834f0c19087002ed00b0 sections=[Insane Voltage (IV) > Progression Goals > Other chemical lines] text="Other chemical lines\nPolybenzimidazole\nAlthough the platline is the foremost line in IV, it is not the only one. The other major lines introduced in IV are the plastic Polybenzimidazole (usually abbreviated to PBI) and various rare earth processing lines used to obtain otherwise rare materials. Although PBI is not strictly required in IV, it is needed for the Tier 2 Thermal Padding that almost every planet from Tier 3 and above demands, and can be used to greatly improve circuit production by making Advanced SMDs, and parallelize multiblocks with the Linked Input Bus.\nRare Earths\nThe rare earth lines are never required, beyond basic samarium processing (which is very short and easy to make, but has poor yield and no byproducts), but are useful for many otherwise rare materials. The simplest of these lines is advanced samarium processing, which greatly improves the yield from Samarium Ore Concentrate Dust and yields byproducts of Cerium, Neodymium, and Lanthanum, and can be upgraded in LuV to further double the Samarium yield. The other two rare earth lines are the Monazite Line (monaline) and the Bastnasite Line (bastline), which can convert the respective crushed ores into a variety of rare earths and other materials: Zirconium, Cerium, Lanthanum, Neodymium, Holmium, Terbium, Gadolinium, and Samarium for the Bastline, and Hafnia, Zirconium, Cerium, Europium, Gadolinium, and Samarium for the Monaline. Of these materials, only Samarium is required to enter LuV, but the others have a variety of uses in LuV and beyond. Note that all of these lines require automating the Dissolution Tank ."
- candidate chunk-2af9ae10450a56bee00cd16589914c6a doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > Overview of Designs] text="Overview of Designs\nOre Processing Design | Tech Tier | Flexible | Expandable | Unbreakable | High Throughput | Low Lag | Minimal Intervention\nOre Processing Design: Limited Flexibility GT Pipes | Tech Tier: LV | Flexible: D | Expandable: F | Unbreakable: C | High Throughput: F | Low Lag: F | Minimal Intervention: C\nOre Processing Design: EnderIO Conduits - The \"Central Line\" | Tech Tier: MV | Flexible: S | Expandable: S | Unbreakable: A | High Throughput: B | Low Lag: A | Minimal Intervention: S\nOre Processing Design: Applied Energistics 2 | Tech Tier: EV | Flexible: SS | Expandable: SS | Unbreakable: S | High Throughput: Setup Dependent | Low Lag: S | Minimal Intervention: S\nOre Processing Design: Ore Processing Multi | Tech Tier: ZPM | Flexible: ? | Expandable: ? | Unbreakable: ? | High Throughput: S | Low Lag: ? | Minimal Intervention: ?"
- candidate chunk-47d289c4d1a14c32f00981954a28a1d8 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > How To Do "Good" Ore Processing? > Flexible] text="Flexible\nOres in GT: New Horizons have multiple \" routes \" you can take which give different outputs. (See the bauxite chart to the right.) For bauxite you can take three routes (Note that these choices simply outline the basic bauxite processing and are not meant to show the \"best\" way to process bauxite, realistically you will probably want to do chembath and/or bauxite slurry):\n- Macerate -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Grossular byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Thermal Centrifuge -> Macerate (Rutile + Gallium byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Simple/Normal Wash -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Rutile + Grossular (if normal wash) byproduct)\nWhile for most bases, Rutile + Gallium Route 2 is superior, perhaps you already have automated gallium from essence berries. Then you might decide to do Route 3 instead, as it gives 2x more Rutile and therefore 2x more Titanium.\nEvery ore in the game will have a different \"optimal\" route for your run and base needs. This may also change over time. As a result, you want ore processing systems to be flexible - allowing different ores to go on different routes.\nYou also want to be able to automatically pull out things at any step of the process. For example, Rare Earths from GT++ require much higher power load, which would cause your existing macerator line to choke without modifications. You'll want to filter these out and process them in specially dedicated macerators."
- evidence chunk-ea1cdf7f833544ff246e6a1d3304aed9 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > HV EnderIO Conduits + GT Type Filters] text="HV EnderIO Conduits + GT Type Filters\nSMRE, System Left Side View\nA fully specified ore processing system designed for GTNH game version 2.1.2.0 using EnderIO Conduit colors with GT Type filters, with all exception item filters up through moon rock processing can be found here on Reddit: Sequential Material Refining Engine (Reddit post has been updated by the author to provide build clarifications. Some recipes are outdated as the GTNH modpack has continued to evolve overtime.)\nSMRE Process Flow Diagram, Game Version 2.1.2.0"
- evidence chunk-d3c0bb92e25671559f4130abe90a4322 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > MV EnderIO Conduits - The "Central Line"] text="MV EnderIO Conduits - The \"Central Line\"\nThis shows the simplest version of a \"central line\" ore processing system. It has one route (priority -2) and is currently useless for anything practical. Gold chest = input and copper chest = output. This shows the second most basic EIO ore processing setup - a central line with an attached macerator round robin module.\nEnderIO conduits are your first and cheapest option for creating a truly flexible ore processing system. This system is built around a \" central conduit line \" which all ores go through, and connected \" modules \" route ores into machines. Because of its modular nature, it is easy to add new routes or expand old ones. This is made possible due to the EIO conduit \" priority \" system, which allows us to make override routes.\nThe priority system for the \" central line \" design goes as follows:\nPriority 0: Override routes. For example, specific ores, rare earths, etc.\nPriority -1: Type filter routes. These are your \"default\" paths that are taken if nothing overrides them.\nPriority -2: Output routes. This goes to the final output chest.\nThe simplest version of this is an input chest set to extract always, some conduits inbetween, and an output chest at -2 priority. Then everything you input (ores, random tinker tools by accident, w/e) will go to the output chest. But obviously this isn't very useful.\nSo you start adding \" modules \" to your inbetween conduits. These provide routes for ores to go. Let's start with a macerator module."

Draconium Ore | 2 | Very Heavy Oil | 0-200\nBauxite | 80 | 8.8%\nApatite | 60 | 6.6%\nKaolinite | 60 | 6.6%\nGarnet Sand & Nether Star | 60 | 6.6%\nTin & Cassiterite | 50 | 5.5%\nRock Salt & Salt | 50 | 5.5%\nBlack Plutonium | 40 | 4.4%\nOilsands | 40 | 4.4%\nQuartzite & Quartz | 20 | 2.2%\nIlmenite | 16 | 1.8%\nChrome | 5 | 0.5%\nOther Notes\nSeaweed needed for Xenoxene covers the floors of the oceans. Dimension required to breed the Seaweed bee.\nT8 Barnard C\nOre Vein | Weight | Frequency | Small Ore | Per Chunk | Fluid Reservoir | Size\nLignite Coal | 160 | 32.9% | Very Heavy Oil | 0-800\nBasaltic Mineral Sand | 80 | 16.5% | Unknown Liquid | 0-300\nArsenic | 60 | 12.3%\nRock Salt & Salt | 50 | 10.3%\nBlack Plutonium | 40 | 8.2%\nOilsands | 40 | 8.2%\nKyanite & Mica | 20 | 4.1%\nTungsten | 16 | 3.3%\nNickel & Osmium | 10 | 2.1%\nSamarium | 10 | 2.1%\nOther Notes\nLogs needed for Radox spawn as trees on the surface. Lakes of Unknown Liquid spawn randomly on the surface. Rainbow Crystals spawn randomly all over the world.\nT8 Barnard E\nOre Vein | Weight | Frequency | Small Ore | Per Chunk | Fluid Reservoir | Size\nMagnetite | 160 | 27.1% | Titanium Ore | 32 | Liquid Air | 0-400\nChalcopyrite & Copper | 80 | 13.5% | Zinc Ore | 24\nGarnet Sand & Nether Star | 60 | 10.2% | Iron Ore | 16\nBentonite & Olivine | 60 | 10.2% | Naquadah Ore | 8\nThorium | 60 | 10.2% | Neutronium Ore | 8\nCadmium & Rare Earth | 40 | 6.8% | Nickel Ore | 8\nGold & Infused Gold | 30 | 5.1% | Black Plutonium Ore | 6\nNaquadah | 30 | 5.1% |"
- candidate chunk-899f089c10d9b05623dd29d55ffafa96 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > EV Applied Energistics 2] text="Lanthanide processing. This processing line takes in crushed Bastnasite or crushed Monazite to yield the different lanthanide elements.\nFor our purposes, we will only need 6 specific AE components in order to create a rudimentary, and yet infinitely expandable AE ore processing system:\n- ME Controller. This is basic and fundamental. You will need at least 1 to be the brains of your ore processing subnet. Each exposed face can connect to and control up to 32 other AE devices, giving our 1-block controller control over a maximum of 192 devices.\n- ME Drive. This will serve as an overflow buffer, just in case all your machines suffer maintenance issues and shutdown simultaneously.\n- ME Storage Cell. Place inside the ME Drive. You will need at least 1.\n- ME Interfaces (the full block type). These will serve as the import blocks, from which the outputs of your machines will be pushed into to enter the AE network. How many you need depends on how creative you are with your GT piping.\n- ME Storage Buses. You will need 1 for each input bus of your multiblock machines, and one extra to handle overflow (optional).\n- ME OreDictionary Filter Cards. This is the magic that makes AE amazing for ore processing. You will need at least 1 per storage bus, with some exceptions.\nNow, let's talk more about the ME OreDictionary Filter Card, or OreDict cards for short. Many GT items have an OreDictionary Name, that looks something like (item type)(name) . There are OreDict names for the ore block, each of the intermediates during the ore processing steps, and the final pure dust. Using galena as an example, they are:\n- Galena Ore. oreGalena\n- Raw Galena Ore. rawOreGalena\n- Crushed Galena Ore. crushedGalena\n- Purified Galena Ore. crushedPurifiedGalena\n- Centrifuged Galena Ore. crushedCentrifugedGalena\n- Impure Pile of Galena Dust dustImpureGalena\n- Purified Pile of Galena Dust dustPureGalena\n- Galena Dust dustGalena\nME Storage Buses attempt to push out compatible items, when immediately received by the network via either ME Interfaces or ME Import Buses, into the inventories that they face. Conventionally, these would be storage blocks like chests/drawers/barrels. But note that Input Buses also have internal inventories. Thus, when we place an OreDict card inside a ME Storage Bus facing towards an Input Bus, we have created an intelligent exporter of items!\nAnother thing to note is that"
- candidate chunk-7204ffb05b8f1f010ede14e35607675b doc=doc-0acf46d9067c834f0c19087002ed00b0 sections=[Insane Voltage (IV) > Progression Goals > Other chemical lines] text="Other chemical lines\nPolybenzimidazole\nAlthough the platline is the foremost line in IV, it is not the only one. The other major lines introduced in IV are the plastic Polybenzimidazole (usually abbreviated to PBI) and various rare earth processing lines used to obtain otherwise rare materials. Although PBI is not strictly required in IV, it is needed for the Tier 2 Thermal Padding that almost every planet from Tier 3 and above demands, and can be used to greatly improve circuit production by making Advanced SMDs, and parallelize multiblocks with the Linked Input Bus.\nRare Earths\nThe rare earth lines are never required, beyond basic samarium processing (which is very short and easy to make, but has poor yield and no byproducts), but are useful for many otherwise rare materials. The simplest of these lines is advanced samarium processing, which greatly improves the yield from Samarium Ore Concentrate Dust and yields byproducts of Cerium, Neodymium, and Lanthanum, and can be upgraded in LuV to further double the Samarium yield. The other two rare earth lines are the Monazite Line (monaline) and the Bastnasite Line (bastline), which can convert the respective crushed ores into a variety of rare earths and other materials: Zirconium, Cerium, Lanthanum, Neodymium, Holmium, Terbium, Gadolinium, and Samarium for the Bastline, and Hafnia, Zirconium, Cerium, Europium, Gadolinium, and Samarium for the Monaline. Of these materials, only Samarium is required to enter LuV, but the others have a variety of uses in LuV and beyond. Note that all of these lines require automating the Dissolution Tank ."
- candidate chunk-c9c1517071de4cd1d65e85ed12a394c8 doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV) > Progression > Side quest 2: Multiblock ore processing] text="Side quest 2: Multiblock ore processing\nAs you entered EV, you gained access to multiblock counterparts of centrifuge, thermal centrifuge, ore washer/chemical bath, and crusher. They are way more efficient compared to their singleblock variants, however they require large amounts of resources like Titanium and most are gated behind TPV coils, so they're listed here instead of in early EV. If your main resource income is from bees, centrifuge is recommended over all other multiblocks as it speeds up centrifuging combs significantly."
- candidate chunk-47d289c4d1a14c32f00981954a28a1d8 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > How To Do "Good" Ore Processing? > Flexible] text="Flexible\nOres in GT: New Horizons have multiple \" routes \" you can take which give different outputs. (See the bauxite chart to the right.) For bauxite you can take three routes (Note that these choices simply outline the basic bauxite processing and are not meant to show the \"best\" way to process bauxite, realistically you will probably want to do chembath and/or bauxite slurry):\n- Macerate -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Grossular byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Thermal Centrifuge -> Macerate (Rutile + Gallium byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Simple/Normal Wash -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Rutile + Grossular (if normal wash) byproduct)\nWhile for most bases, Rutile + Gallium Route 2 is superior, perhaps you already have automated gallium from essence berries. Then you might decide to do Route 3 instead, as it gives 2x more Rutile and therefore 2x more Titanium.\nEvery ore in the game will have a different \"optimal\" route for your run and base needs. This may also change over time. As a result, you want ore processing systems to be flexible - allowing different ores to go on different routes.\nYou also want to be able to automatically pull out things at any step of the process. For example, Rare Earths from GT++ require much higher power load, which would cause your existing macerator line to choke without modifications. You'll want to filter these out and process them in specially dedicated macerators."
- candidate chunk-b91cca541a48a644cc92102fc6a3381b doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > EV Applied Energistics 2] text="necessary in order to handle the large amounts of ores obtained by advanced Ore Drilling Plants and Void Miners.\nAn alternative to the ASIB in pre-LuV is the combination of the usage of the ME Export Bus with an ME OreDictionary Filter card set with the filter of \"*\", emulating the stocking of the ASIB by pushing the storage of the subnetwork to the input buses. However, a downside is that there is no benefit of immediate recipe checks unlike the ASIB, and the size of the input is limited to the slots of the input bus.\nAnd like this, you now have an AE-linked ore processing machine capable of:\n- Accepting both crushed ores (from ODPs) or whole ores (from VMs).\n- Sifting appropriate ores capable of gem byproducts.\n- Acquisition of tertiary byproducts from all possible ores (via thermal centrifuging and macerating of centrifuged ores)\n- Export of dusts, gems (of all qualities), purified sphalerite and galena ores for indium processing, and crushed bastnasite and monazite for lanthanide processing.\nAuxiliary ore processing setups that we can link to our main network, along with the useful OreDicts, are:\n- Industrial Centrifuges to process Stone Dust ( dustStone ), Impure dusts ( dustImpure* ), Limonites ( dust*Limonite ), Metal Mixture Dust ( dustMetalMixture ), Sheldonite dusts ( dustCooperite ), and others\n- Industrial Electrolyzers to process arbitrary dusts to recover purified elemental dusts and gases\n- LCRs to process purified Sphalerite and purified Galena with sulfuric acid to get Indium Concentrate, and the subsequent precipitation using Aluminium dust to get Indium Dust and Lead-Zinc solution (can be electrolyzed to recover lead, silver, zinc, sulfur and water)\n- OWP set to Chemical Bath mode with a screwdriver to process Rare Earth dusts with sulfuric acid and get Crushed Rare Earth ores. Feed these into the ore processing network to eventually recover Rare Earth (I) dusts which can be dehydrated to recover useful dusts (namely Yttrium).\n- Platinum Processing Line , fed with the Platinum Metallic Powder Dust. Too long and complex to catalog here.\n- Lanthanide Processing Line, fed with the crushed Bastnasite and crushed Monazite ores. Too long to catalog here."
- evidence chunk-d3c0bb92e25671559f4130abe90a4322 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > MV EnderIO Conduits - The "Central Line"] text="MV EnderIO Conduits - The \"Central Line\"\nThis shows the simplest version of a \"central line\" ore processing system. It has one route (priority -2) and is currently useless for anything practical. Gold chest = input and copper chest = output. This shows the second most basic EIO ore processing setup - a central line with an attached macerator round robin module.\nEnderIO conduits are your first and cheapest option for creating a truly flexible ore processing system. This system is built around a \" central conduit line \" which all ores go through, and connected \" modules \" route ores into machines. Because of its modular nature, it is easy to add new routes or expand old ones. This is made possible due to the EIO conduit \" priority \" system, which allows us to make override routes.\nThe priority system for the \" central line \" design goes as follows:\nPriority 0: Override routes. For example, specific ores, rare earths, etc.\nPriority -1: Type filter routes. These are your \"default\" paths that are taken if nothing overrides them.\nPriority -2: Output routes. This goes to the final output chest.\nThe simplest version of this is an input chest set to extract always, some conduits inbetween, and an output chest at -2 priority. Then everything you input (ores, random tinker tools by accident, w/e) will go to the output chest. But obviously this isn't very useful.\nSo you start adding \" modules \" to your inbetween conduits. These provide routes for ores to go. Let's start with a macerator module."
- evidence chunk-ea1cdf7f833544ff246e6a1d3304aed9 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > HV EnderIO Conduits + GT Type Filters] text="HV EnderIO Conduits + GT Type Filters\nSMRE, System Left Side View\nA fully specified ore processing system designed for GTNH game version 2.1.2.0 using EnderIO Conduit colors with GT Type filters, with all exception item filters up through moon rock processing can be found here on Reddit: Sequential Material Refining Engine (Reddit post has been updated by the author to provide build clarifications. Some recipes are outdated as the GTNH modpack has continued to evolve overtime.)\nSMRE Process Flow Diagram, Game Version 2.1.2.0"
- evidence chunk-06d40bd7fed30e57f17a1a25576898d6 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > EV Applied Energistics 2] text="EV Applied Energistics 2\nApplied Energistics (henceforth just referred to as AE) is the most commonly used logistics system for mid-late game. As such, it finds common use in high-tier ore-processing solutions designed by players. From EV - ZPM, the multiblock miners ( Ore Drilling Plants I/II/III/IV)(ODPs) become available. At IV and beyond (up to a point), single-block machines are no longer acceptable for the amount of ores we will be processing, and instead we will move on to the GT++ multiblock machines. These are:\n- Ore Washing Plant (OWP)\n- Maceration Stack (MS)\n- Large Thermal Refinery (LTR). This is the multiblock thermal centrifuge.\n- Industrial Centrifuge (IC)\n- Large Sifter (LS).\n- Industrial Electrolyzer (IE)\n- Utupu-Tanuri / Dehydrator (only necessary for Rare Earths and a few Toxic Everglades ores)\nOther multiblock machines that we will be using are the Large Chemical Reactors (LCRs).\nFor a typical ore processing setup at EV - ZPM, we generally have the following desires.\n- Maximise returns. If we throw in an ore that has tertiary byproducts, we want all the byproducts. This requires use of a thermal centrifuge. Thus, our system will make use of thermal centrifuges, especially for gallium\n- Maximise throughput. ODPs are capable of immense output, and an inadequate setup will choke and die. In addition, Void Miners (VMs) become available near the tail end of this period, and these will overwhelm unprepared systems, especially when boosted with the noble gases.\n- Indium retrieval. Indium is a particular metal needed for specific circuit components (namely as indium gallium phosphide, or InGaP). There are various ways to get this metal. Replication with UU-Matter (slow and painful), fusion from lithium and silver (requires T3 Fusion , unlocked in UV ), milling sphalerite (unlocked in LuV ), processing Naquadria (unlocked in LuV ), processing Roquesite or pure Indium ore (extremely rare) or extraction from purified Sphalerite and Galena ores .\n- Lanthanide processing. This processing line takes in crushed Bastnasite or crushed Monazite to yield the different lanthanide elements.\nFor our purposes, we will only need 6 specific AE components in order to create a rudimentary, and yet infinitely expandable AE ore processing system:\n- ME Controller. This is basic and fundamental. You will need at least 1 to be the brains of your ore processing subnet. Each exposed face can connect to and control up to 32 other AE devices,"

It only describes the EnderIO central-line priority scheme (Priority 0 override routes, Priority -1 type filter/default routes, Priority -2 output routes) and, separately, that a thermal centrifuge is used especially for gallium. - candidate chunk-eba529c2606e0f9cb9b5b4980bcfc289 doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV) > Side Objectives > The End and pre-Moon Titanium] text="The End and pre-Moon Titanium\nDiagram showing processing steps for making Titanium Ingots from Naquadah Ore. EBF - Electric Blast Furnace; LCR - Large Chemical Reactor; DT - Distillation Tower.\nEyes of Ender can be made in an HV Chemical Reactor and used to find and activate the End Portal. After defeating the Ender Dragon, the player can travel to the Far End Asteroids where they will occasionally find veins of Naquadah Ore. This allows the player to produce Titanium Ingots without traveling to the Moon.\nRaw Naquadah Ore goes through the usual ore processing and becomes Naquadah Oxide Mixture Dust. Two dust can be processed in the Electric Blast Furnace with 3000L of Fluoroantimonic Acid to produce 4 Titanium Trifluoride Dust, which is then put back into the EBF, which must be overclocked to EV , with 3000L of Hydrogen to get a Hot Titanium Ingot. Cool the ingot in a Vacuum Freezer to obtain a Titanium Ingot.\nThe production of Fluoroantimonic Acid is an involved process which requires a Large Chemical Reactor that is overclocked to EV. A Distillation Tower is not necessary for the production of Antimony Pentachloride but will refund all of the Ether used in the production of Antimony Trichloride Solution.\nThe diagram to the right shows the processing steps for obtaining Titanium Ingots from Naquadah Ore. Note that Ethylene and Hydrogen Sulfide are considered inputs, rather than being part of the production chain, as they can be obtained in great quantities from processing Oil and its byproducts."

- candidate chunk-8f38ce3e38a37b3091fc7e45fcea268c doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV) > Progression > Side Quests] text="Side Quests\n-Eliminate microcrafting and improve automation and QoL with AE2 .\n-Build advanced processing lines for Ilmenite and Bauxite.\n-Make a Multiblock Miner for higher ore output.\n-Make various GT++ Multiblock versions of singleblocks such as the Bending Machine, Centrifuge, Lathe and Maceration Stack."
- candidate chunk-d0feb569b22562ab917c598f227b6810 doc=doc-0acf46d9067c834f0c19087002ed00b0 sections=[Insane Voltage (IV) > Progression Goals > The Platline] text="The Platline\nThe Platinum Line , known as the Platline for short, is the central chemical line of IV, and is one of the most complex chemical lines in the entire pack, with approximately 50 steps in total. Although the first part of the platline, which yields Platinum itself, should be done already in EV, the rest, for Palladium, Rhodium, Ruthenium, Iridium, and Osmium, is required to progress into further tiers as well as for many useful upgrades and quality of life improvements within IV. These include the IV Energy Hatch, the Infinite Spray Can (a spray can with access to all 16 colors and infinite uses, for coloring cables), the Vajra (a tool to instantly break any block), and the HSS-S Coils for upgrading the Electric Blast Furnace and other multiblocks. It is possible to run the platline manually as well, in order to obtain small amounts of platline materials to afford key upgrades without needing to build the entire line immediately; for example, an IV Energy Hatch or two to be able to run certain otherwise impossible recipes, such as Nitinol 60 in an ABS to be able to make a HILE ."
- candidate chunk-47d289c4d1a14c32f00981954a28a1d8 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > How To Do "Good" Ore Processing? > Flexible] text="Flexible\nOres in GT: New Horizons have multiple \" routes \" you can take which give different outputs. (See the bauxite chart to the right.) For bauxite you can take three routes (Note that these choices simply outline the basic bauxite processing and are not meant to show the \"best\" way to process bauxite, realistically you will probably want to do chembath and/or bauxite slurry):\n- Macerate -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Grossular byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Thermal Centrifuge -> Macerate (Rutile + Gallium byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Simple/Normal Wash -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Rutile + Grossular (if normal wash) byproduct)\nWhile for most bases, Rutile + Gallium Route 2 is superior, perhaps you already have automated gallium from essence berries. Then you might decide to do Route 3 instead, as it gives 2x more Rutile and therefore 2x more Titanium.\nEvery ore in the game will have a different \"optimal\" route for your run and base needs. This may also change over time. As a result, you want ore processing systems to be flexible - allowing different ores to go on different routes.\nYou also want to be able to automatically pull out things at any step of the process. For example, Rare Earths from GT++ require much higher power load, which would cause your existing macerator line to choke without modifications. You'll want to filter these out and process them in specially dedicated macerators."
- evidence chunk-d3c0bb92e25671559f4130abe90a4322 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > MV EnderIO Conduits - The "Central Line"] text="MV EnderIO Conduits - The \"Central Line\"\nThis shows the simplest version of a \"central line\" ore processing system. It has one route (priority -2) and is currently useless for anything practical. Gold chest = input and copper chest = output. This shows the second most basic EIO ore processing setup - a central line with an attached macerator round robin module.\nEnderIO conduits are your first and cheapest option for creating a truly flexible ore processing system. This system is built around a \" central conduit line \" which all ores go through, and connected \" modules \" route ores into machines. Because of its modular nature, it is easy to add new routes or expand old ones. This is made possible due to the EIO conduit \" priority \" system, which allows us to make override routes.\nThe priority system for the \" central line \" design goes as follows:\nPriority 0: Override routes. For example, specific ores, rare earths, etc.\nPriority -1: Type filter routes. These are your \"default\" paths that are taken if nothing overrides them.\nPriority -2: Output routes. This goes to the final output chest.\nThe simplest version of this is an input chest set to extract always, some conduits inbetween, and an output chest at -2 priority. Then everything you input (ores, random tinker tools by accident, w/e) will go to the output chest. But obviously this isn't very useful.\nSo you start adding \" modules \" to your inbetween conduits. These provide routes for ores to go. Let's start with a macerator module."
- evidence chunk-ea1cdf7f833544ff246e6a1d3304aed9 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > HV EnderIO Conduits + GT Type Filters] text="HV EnderIO Conduits + GT Type Filters\nSMRE, System Left Side View\nA fully specified ore processing system designed for GTNH game version 2.1.2.0 using EnderIO Conduit colors with GT Type filters, with all exception item filters up through moon rock processing can be found here on Reddit: Sequential Material Refining Engine (Reddit post has been updated by the author to provide build clarifications. Some recipes are outdated as the GTNH modpack has continued to evolve overtime.)\nSMRE Process Flow Diagram, Game Version 2.1.2.0"
- evidence chunk-06d40bd7fed30e57f17a1a25576898d6 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > EV Applied Energistics 2] text="EV Applied Energistics 2\nApplied Energistics (henceforth just referred to as AE) is the most commonly used logistics system for mid-late game. As such, it finds common use in high-tier ore-processing solutions designed by players. From EV - ZPM, the multiblock miners ( Ore Drilling Plants I/II/III/IV)(ODPs) become available. At IV and beyond (up to a point), single-block machines are no longer acceptable for the amount of ores we will be processing, and instead we will move on to the GT++ multiblock machines. These are:\n- Ore Washing Plant (OWP)\n- Maceration Stack (MS)\n- Large Thermal Refinery (LTR). This is the multiblock thermal centrifuge.\n- Industrial Centrifuge (IC)\n- Large Sifter (LS).\n- Industrial Electrolyzer (IE)\n- Utupu-Tanuri / Dehydrator (only necessary for Rare Earths and a few Toxic Everglades ores)\nOther multiblock machines that we will be using are the Large Chemical Reactors (LCRs).\nFor a typical ore processing setup at EV - ZPM, we generally have the following desires.\n- Maximise returns. If we throw in an ore that has tertiary byproducts, we want all the byproducts. This requires use of a thermal centrifuge. Thus, our system will make use of thermal centrifuges, especially for gallium\n- Maximise throughput. ODPs are capable of immense output, and an inadequate setup will choke and die. In addition, Void Miners (VMs) become available near the tail end of this period, and these will overwhelm unprepared systems, especially when boosted with the noble gases.\n- Indium retrieval. Indium is a particular metal needed for specific circuit components (namely as indium gallium phosphide, or InGaP). There are various ways to get this metal. Replication with UU-Matter (slow and painful), fusion from lithium and silver (requires T3 Fusion , unlocked in UV ), milling sphalerite (unlocked in LuV ), processing Naquadria (unlocked in LuV ), processing Roquesite or pure Indium ore (extremely rare) or extraction from purified Sphalerite and Galena ores .\n- Lanthanide processing. This processing line takes in crushed Bastnasite or crushed Monazite to yield the different lanthanide elements.\nFor our purposes, we will only need 6 specific AE components in order to create a rudimentary, and yet infinitely expandable AE ore processing system:\n- ME Controller. This is basic and fundamental. You will need at least 1 to be the brains of your ore processing subnet. Each exposed face can connect to and control up to 32 other AE devices,"

- candidate chunk-d3c0bb92e25671559f4130abe90a4322 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > MV EnderIO Conduits - The "Central Line"] text="MV EnderIO Conduits - The \"Central Line\"\nThis shows the simplest version of a \"central line\" ore processing system. It has one route (priority -2) and is currently useless for anything practical. Gold chest = input and copper chest = output. This shows the second most basic EIO ore processing setup - a central line with an attached macerator round robin module.\nEnderIO conduits are your first and cheapest option for creating a truly flexible ore processing system. This system is built around a \" central conduit line \" which all ores go through, and connected \" modules \" route ores into machines. Because of its modular nature, it is easy to add new routes or expand old ones. This is made possible due to the EIO conduit \" priority \" system, which allows us to make override routes.\nThe priority system for the \" central line \" design goes as follows:\nPriority 0: Override routes. For example, specific ores, rare earths, etc.\nPriority -1: Type filter routes. These are your \"default\" paths that are taken if nothing overrides them.\nPriority -2: Output routes. This goes to the final output chest.\nThe simplest version of this is an input chest set to extract always, some conduits inbetween, and an output chest at -2 priority. Then everything you input (ores, random tinker tools by accident, w/e) will go to the output chest. But obviously this isn't very useful.\nSo you start adding \" modules \" to your inbetween conduits. These provide routes for ores to go. Let's start with a macerator module."
- candidate chunk-ea1cdf7f833544ff246e6a1d3304aed9 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > HV EnderIO Conduits + GT Type Filters] text="HV EnderIO Conduits + GT Type Filters\nSMRE, System Left Side View\nA fully specified ore processing system designed for GTNH game version 2.1.2.0 using EnderIO Conduit colors with GT Type filters, with all exception item filters up through moon rock processing can be found here on Reddit: Sequential Material Refining Engine (Reddit post has been updated by the author to provide build clarifications. Some recipes are outdated as the GTNH modpack has continued to evolve overtime.)\nSMRE Process Flow Diagram, Game Version 2.1.2.0"
- candidate chunk-47d289c4d1a14c32f00981954a28a1d8 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > How To Do "Good" Ore Processing? > Flexible] text="Flexible\nOres in GT: New Horizons have multiple \" routes \" you can take which give different outputs. (See the bauxite chart to the right.) For bauxite you can take three routes (Note that these choices simply outline the basic bauxite processing and are not meant to show the \"best\" way to process bauxite, realistically you will probably want to do chembath and/or bauxite slurry):\n- Macerate -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Grossular byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Thermal Centrifuge -> Macerate (Rutile + Gallium byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Simple/Normal Wash -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Rutile + Grossular (if normal wash) byproduct)\nWhile for most bases, Rutile + Gallium Route 2 is superior, perhaps you already have automated gallium from essence berries. Then you might decide to do Route 3 instead, as it gives 2x more Rutile and therefore 2x more Titanium.\nEvery ore in the game will have a different \"optimal\" route for your run and base needs. This may also change over time. As a result, you want ore processing systems to be flexible - allowing different ores to go on different routes.\nYou also want to be able to automatically pull out things at any step of the process. For example, Rare Earths from GT++ require much higher power load, which would cause your existing macerator line to choke without modifications. You'll want to filter these out and process them in specially dedicated macerators."
- candidate chunk-48821e45dbe567c3c4946b397bd05ac8 doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > Progression] text="Progression\nProgression to MV requires the player to obtain 3 essential materials:\n• Gallium -\nThere is no renewable way to obtain Gallium in LV. The best way is to electrolyze Sphalerite dust, giving an 11% chance for Gallium. Gallium is also obtainable as a byproduct of Zinc ore processing, Zinc Gravel can be purchased with Blacksmith coins at regular enough intervals that it shouldn't be a major issue for now. You can also use XP buckets if you're desperate. With an HV macerator you can obtain more byproducts from ore processing, making Gallium much easier to obtain.\n• Arsenic -\nCobaltite is the primary way of obtaining Arsenic in LV. When Cobaltite is blasted in an EBF with oxygen it produces roasted cobalt and arsenic dust. Running roasted arsenic dust through the EBF again results in 3 arsenic ingots per 2 cobaltite. This process is oxygen neutral after electrolyzing the carbon dioxide produced.\n• Aluminum -\nAluminum has 2 ways to be obtained in LV, one involves compressing Aluminum dust and blasting the \"raw Aluminum\" into 1 Aluminum ingot. This recipe is slow and not recommended. The other recipe uses 10 Alumina and 5 cryolite to produce 4 Aluminum ingots. While being more involved, this recipe offers much faster and more power efficient Aluminum."
- candidate chunk-eba529c2606e0f9cb9b5b4980bcfc289 doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV) > Side Objectives > The End and pre-Moon Titanium] text="The End and pre-Moon Titanium\nDiagram showing processing steps for making Titanium Ingots from Naquadah Ore. EBF - Electric Blast Furnace; LCR - Large Chemical Reactor; DT - Distillation Tower.\nEyes of Ender can be made in an HV Chemical Reactor and used to find and activate the End Portal. After defeating the Ender Dragon, the player can travel to the Far End Asteroids where they will occasionally find veins of Naquadah Ore. This allows the player to produce Titanium Ingots without traveling to the Moon.\nRaw Naquadah Ore goes through the usual ore processing and becomes Naquadah Oxide Mixture Dust. Two dust can be processed in the Electric Blast Furnace with 3000L of Fluoroantimonic Acid to produce 4 Titanium Trifluoride Dust, which is then put back into the EBF, which must be overclocked to EV , with 3000L of Hydrogen to get a Hot Titanium Ingot. Cool the ingot in a Vacuum Freezer to obtain a Titanium Ingot.\nThe production of Fluoroantimonic Acid is an involved process which requires a Large Chemical Reactor that is overclocked to EV. A Distillation Tower is not necessary for the production of Antimony Pentachloride but will refund all of the Ether used in the production of Antimony Trichloride Solution.\nThe diagram to the right shows the processing steps for obtaining Titanium Ingots from Naquadah Ore. Note that Ethylene and Hydrogen Sulfide are considered inputs, rather than being part of the production chain, as they can be obtained in great quantities from processing Oil and its byproducts."
- candidate chunk-06d40bd7fed30e57f17a1a25576898d6 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > EV Applied Energistics 2] text="EV Applied Energistics 2\nApplied Energistics (henceforth just referred to as AE) is the most commonly used logistics system for mid-late game. As such, it finds common use in high-tier ore-processing solutions designed by players. From EV - ZPM, the multiblock miners ( Ore Drilling Plants I/II/III/IV)(ODPs) become available. At IV and beyond (up to a point), single-block machines are no longer acceptable for the amount of ores we will be processing, and instead we will move on to the GT++ multiblock machines. These are:\n- Ore Washing Plant (OWP)\n- Maceration Stack (MS)\n- Large Thermal Refinery (LTR). This is the multiblock thermal centrifuge.\n- Industrial Centrifuge (IC)\n- Large Sifter (LS).\n- Industrial Electrolyzer (IE)\n- Utupu-Tanuri / Dehydrator (only necessary for Rare Earths and a few Toxic Everglades ores)\nOther multiblock machines that we will be using are the Large Chemical Reactors (LCRs).\nFor a typical ore processing setup at EV - ZPM, we generally have the following desires.\n- Maximise returns. If we throw in an ore that has tertiary byproducts, we want all the byproducts. This requires use of a thermal centrifuge. Thus, our system will make use of thermal centrifuges, especially for gallium\n- Maximise throughput. ODPs are capable of immense output, and an inadequate setup will choke and die. In addition, Void Miners (VMs) become available near the tail end of this period, and these will overwhelm unprepared systems, especially when boosted with the noble gases.\n- Indium retrieval. Indium is a particular metal needed for specific circuit components (namely as indium gallium phosphide, or InGaP). There are various ways to get this metal. Replication with UU-Matter (slow and painful), fusion from lithium and silver (requires T3 Fusion , unlocked in UV ), milling sphalerite (unlocked in LuV ), processing Naquadria (unlocked in LuV ), processing Roquesite or pure Indium ore (extremely rare) or extraction from purified Sphalerite and Galena ores .\n- Lanthanide processing. This processing line takes in crushed Bastnasite or crushed Monazite to yield the different lanthanide elements.\nFor our purposes, we will only need 6 specific AE components in order to create a rudimentary, and yet infinitely expandable AE ore processing system:\n- ME Controller. This is basic and fundamental. You will need at least 1 to be the brains of your ore processing subnet. Each exposed face can connect to and control up to 32 other AE devices,"

- candidate chunk-6bebc7c1c54fce837efb57613020c769 doc=doc-c9afb2e028c318ddc2f228663f5523fe sections=[Extremely Ultimate Voltage (UEV) > Progression > Tengam] text="Tengam\nThe first step in UEV progression is using the Mothership to travel to Seth , which is the only planet where Raw Tengam Ore can be found. - candidate chunk-d0feb569b22562ab917c598f227b6810 doc=doc-0acf46d9067c834f0c19087002ed00b0 sections=[Insane Voltage (IV) > Progression Goals > The Platline] text="The Platline\nThe Platinum Line , known as the Platline for short, is the central chemical line of IV, and is one of the most complex chemical lines in the entire pack, with approximately 50 steps in total. Although the first part of the platline, which yields Platinum itself, should be done already in EV, the rest, for Palladium, Rhodium, Ruthenium, Iridium, and Osmium, is required to progress into further tiers as well as for many useful upgrades and quality of life improvements within IV. These include the IV Energy Hatch, the Infinite Spray Can (a spray can with access to all 16 colors and infinite uses, for coloring cables), the Vajra (a tool to instantly break any block), and the HSS-S Coils for upgrading the Electric Blast Furnace and other multiblocks. It is possible to run the platline manually as well, in order to obtain small amounts of platline materials to afford key upgrades without needing to build the entire line immediately; for example, an IV Energy Hatch or two to be able to run certain otherwise impossible recipes, such as Nitinol 60 in an ABS to be able to make a HILE ."
- candidate chunk-c7fe9475868cd154a1c7e73956ed4139 doc=doc-04ef7ad9e1765bcf95cbd804c813863b sections=[Ultimate Voltage (UV) > Infrastructure Improvements > Component Assembly Line] text="Component Assembly Line\nThe Component Assembly Line (CoAL) is a new multiblock for assembling components, such as Electric Motors, Robot Arms, and Sensors. It provides a 25% discount on both resources and time, as well as skipping the Assembly Line , two incredible advantages that mean it should be the only source of components whenever it can be used. The main cost of the CoAL is the 43 tiered Component Assembly Line Casings that determine the maximum available tier of components: a full CoAL takes 688 Electric Motors, 430 Electric Pistons, and 344 Robot Arms of its tier. In UV, both the UV and ZPM CoAL tiers are viable. The ZPM CoAL doesn't help with UV components, but it is much cheaper than the UV CoAL, and still useful for LuV and ZPM superconductors in particular, which are used for Fusion Reactor coils and Wetware Mainframes. The UV CoAL adds the ability to make UV components, but is very expensive, particularly on Americium, until late in the tier."
- candidate chunk-137405157af87dd0f3eee24e6971f51d doc=doc-626c925b618145e4e43655f7673b36b4 sections=[Low Voltage (LV) > GT Electric Machines] text="GT Electric Machines\nMachine GUI. Auto-output buttons circled in yellow.\nLV Electric machines have some new features that were not available with Steam-age machinery. For example, the steam exhaust is replaced with an automatic output. This output's facing can be changed with a wrench similar to how the exhaust was configured on Steam machines. Just like their Steam counterparts, all electric GT machines cannot be input or output from their front face. To enable automatic output, open the GUI of the machine and click on the button at the lower left corner. There are two automatic output toggle buttons; the left one is for liquid and the right one is for items.\nElectrolyzer clay dust recipe. The MV power requirement is circled in yellow.\nGregTech machinery is divided by tier, which corresponds to a specific voltage - LV (32 V), MV (128 V), HV (512 V) etc. Most recipes are gated to a specific voltage/tier or higher, which is shown with the recipe in NEI (Not Enough Items). If the recipe requires more than one amp it will also be listed here. Pay careful attention to what tier a recipe requires - with LV machinery, MV and higher recipes are inaccessible.\nFor example, putting Clay Dust through the Electrolyzer to get Aluminium dust requires 120 EU/t (MV). Since the Basic Electrolyzer is only able to do 32EU/t, this recipe is unavailable until the Advanced Electrolyzer (MV).\nRecipes close to the voltage limit for LV (32 EU/t) may cause the machine to request multiple amps, especially if there is any cable loss. A single LV generator will often not be capable of supplying enough power if the machine is placed too far away. Most GT machines will accept up to 2 amps. A second generator or Battery Buffer may be needed to keep a a high cost recipe running. If a machine runs out of power, it will stall resetting any progress but not losing the materials. A soft mallet can be used to temporarily disable a machine to give a buffer time to recharge or wait until more power generation is available.\nSome machines require multiple amps to run at all, such as the Arc Furnace and Electric Blast Furnace . The Arc Furnace requires 3 amps to function and can accept up to 7 amps. Checking any recipe for the Arc Furnace will show Usage XX/t, voltage 32 EU/t"
- candidate chunk-c9be8e2d914b8716772e72eed1d32b83 doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV) > Progression > Mid-HV] text="Mid-HV\nWith a more powerful energy infrastructure setup and running Cleanroom, you can start working on EV circuits, perfect for creating the HV Circuit Assembler. The HV Circuit Assembler allows you to make IV circuits, which are necessary for the NASA Workbench. From now on, your copper consumption will greatly increase due to the massive demand from plastic circuit boards.\nYou will also want to look at the new multiblocks such as the Large Chemical Reactor, Distillation Tower, Oil Cracker, and Vacuum Freezer.\nThe Large Chemical Reactor might not be absolutely mandatory yet, but has some huge benefits: it has perfect overclocks, meaning that speed will quadruple every tier with ZERO additional power cost, it allows you to stop using cells for liquids, and it gates many extremely useful recipes such as a two-step PTFE recipe (for more LCRs) and one-step sulfuric acid from sulfur. The Distillation Tower will be your most expensive multiblock (requiring about ~56 Stainless Steel ingots for every layer), but the added efficiency in both resource production and energy consumption will make it absolutely worth it in the long run.\nThe Vacuum Freezer is not absolutely necessary until whenever you need Nichrome coils, but is worth crafting sooner than later. It is much cheaper to craft than you might expect (hopefully you have at least 2 and a half stacks of aluminium laying around at this point?) and massively reduces time and energy consumption for whatever Hot Ingots you may have originally been cooling using the Chemical Bath.\nThe Multi Smelter looks similar to an EBF but requires half the coils. It has insanely good efficiency and parallels while also being pretty cheap at this point, so it is highly recommended to get. You will most likely only need to build one of this.\nLastly, there are some multiblocks not mentioned previously that are possible to craft now that you have a cleanroom up and running:\n- Oil Cracking Unit : Replaces the hydrogen and steam cracking recipes that were done in the Chemical Reactor in previous tiers. It provides power cost reduction and bonus outputs. This is absolutely worth it for anyone using Oil to produce chemicals such as Ethylene.\n- Large Sifter : The first unlockable ore processor. It provides a massive boost to speed in the form of raw time reduction as well as parallels. In addition, sifting is a mandatory step in"
- candidate chunk-feab760150faebb9f605a3a73090de9b doc=doc-16afb764b518d80604353589d360da21 sections=[Medium Voltage (MV) > Goals > Optional Side Quests] text="Optional Side Quests\n- Aluminium & defeating the first Twilight Forest boss Naga, which gives the player access to Thaumcraft .\n- Create the Good Miner for additional mining range and fortune bonus. Remember that it can be powered by LV generators.\n- Use EnderIO Item Conduits to instantly move things around complex networks. The EnderIO conduits have additional bonuses, such as routing, support for item filtering, and the ability to pull from non-output machine sides, but have less throughput without upgrades.\n- Use the Tool Forge to fill out your set of Tinker's Construct Hammer/Excavator/Scythe/Cleaver and other tools.\n- Convert single-block steam/LV machines to their MV counterparts, or upgrade to Steam multiblocks ( Steam Oven , Steam Grinder , Steam Squasher , etc).\n- Start Bees or IC2 Crops for renewable resources.\n- Botania & Witchery."
- candidate chunk-9706297da3128f57a7f1b9dde7e785df doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > How To Do "Good" Ore Processing? > Expandable] text="Expandable\nDepending on what batch you are processing, you may find a major bottleneck in your current system, or you simply want to replace machines with multis. This means your system must be able to expand without causing catastrophic infrastructure redesigns. Ideally you only want to change stuff in the current step you're dealing with (eg thermal centrifuging), not the whole line."
- candidate chunk-a61e46971fad35640888a9436665f7b2 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > MV EnderIO Conduits - The "Central Line" > The Rest of the Line] text="The Rest of the Line\nEvery module you add will look almost exactly like the macerator module. The only things you will swap out will be different filters and machines in place of the macerators.\nFor specific item overrides (eg. I want crushed bauxite specifically to go to thermal centrifuging), remember to use priority 0, as this will override items going to the normal macerator route we defined above.\nOn the right you can see an example of a fully fledged EIO ore processing line."
- evidence chunk-d3c0bb92e25671559f4130abe90a4322 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > MV EnderIO Conduits - The "Central Line"] text="MV EnderIO Conduits - The \"Central Line\"\nThis shows the simplest version of a \"central line\" ore processing system. It has one route (priority -2) and is currently useless for anything practical. Gold chest = input and copper chest = output. This shows the second most basic EIO ore processing setup - a central line with an attached macerator round robin module.\nEnderIO conduits are your first and cheapest option for creating a truly flexible ore processing system. This system is built around a \" central conduit line \" which all ores go through, and connected \" modules \" route ores into machines. Because of its modular nature, it is easy to add new routes or expand old ones. This is made possible due to the EIO conduit \" priority \" system, which allows us to make override routes.\nThe priority system for the \" central line \" design goes as follows:\nPriority 0: Override routes. For example, specific ores, rare earths, etc.\nPriority -1: Type filter routes. These are your \"default\" paths that are taken if nothing overrides them.\nPriority -2: Output routes. This goes to the final output chest.\nThe simplest version of this is an input chest set to extract always, some conduits inbetween, and an output chest at -2 priority. Then everything you input (ores, random tinker tools by accident, w/e) will go to the output chest. But obviously this isn't very useful.\nSo you start adding \" modules \" to your inbetween conduits. These provide routes for ores to go. Let's start with a macerator module."

- candidate chunk-06d40bd7fed30e57f17a1a25576898d6 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > EV Applied Energistics 2] text="EV Applied Energistics 2\nApplied Energistics (henceforth just referred to as AE) is the most commonly used logistics system for mid-late game. As such, it finds common use in high-tier ore-processing solutions designed by players. From EV - ZPM, the multiblock miners ( Ore Drilling Plants I/II/III/IV)(ODPs) become available. At IV and beyond (up to a point), single-block machines are no longer acceptable for the amount of ores we will be processing, and instead we will move on to the GT++ multiblock machines. These are:\n- Ore Washing Plant (OWP)\n- Maceration Stack (MS)\n- Large Thermal Refinery (LTR). This is the multiblock thermal centrifuge.\n- Industrial Centrifuge (IC)\n- Large Sifter (LS).\n- Industrial Electrolyzer (IE)\n- Utupu-Tanuri / Dehydrator (only necessary for Rare Earths and a few Toxic Everglades ores)\nOther multiblock machines that we will be using are the Large Chemical Reactors (LCRs).\nFor a typical ore processing setup at EV - ZPM, we generally have the following desires.\n- Maximise returns. If we throw in an ore that has tertiary byproducts, we want all the byproducts. This requires use of a thermal centrifuge. Thus, our system will make use of thermal centrifuges, especially for gallium\n- Maximise throughput. ODPs are capable of immense output, and an inadequate setup will choke and die. In addition, Void Miners (VMs) become available near the tail end of this period, and these will overwhelm unprepared systems, especially when boosted with the noble gases.\n- Indium retrieval. Indium is a particular metal needed for specific circuit components (namely as indium gallium phosphide, or InGaP). There are various ways to get this metal. Replication with UU-Matter (slow and painful), fusion from lithium and silver (requires T3 Fusion , unlocked in UV ), milling sphalerite (unlocked in LuV ), processing Naquadria (unlocked in LuV ), processing Roquesite or pure Indium ore (extremely rare) or extraction from purified Sphalerite and Galena ores .\n- Lanthanide processing. This processing line takes in crushed Bastnasite or crushed Monazite to yield the different lanthanide elements.\nFor our purposes, we will only need 6 specific AE components in order to create a rudimentary, and yet infinitely expandable AE ore processing system:\n- ME Controller. This is basic and fundamental. You will need at least 1 to be the brains of your ore processing subnet. Each exposed face can connect to and control up to 32 other AE devices,"
- candidate chunk-c9c1517071de4cd1d65e85ed12a394c8 doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV) > Progression > Side quest 2: Multiblock ore processing] text="Side quest 2: Multiblock ore processing\nAs you entered EV, you gained access to multiblock counterparts of centrifuge, thermal centrifuge, ore washer/chemical bath, and crusher. They are way more efficient compared to their singleblock variants, however they require large amounts of resources like Titanium and most are gated behind TPV coils, so they're listed here instead of in early EV. If your main resource income is from bees, centrifuge is recommended over all other multiblocks as it speeds up centrifuging combs significantly."
- candidate chunk-47d289c4d1a14c32f00981954a28a1d8 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > How To Do "Good" Ore Processing? > Flexible] text="Flexible\nOres in GT: New Horizons have multiple \" routes \" you can take which give different outputs. (See the bauxite chart to the right.) For bauxite you can take three routes (Note that these choices simply outline the basic bauxite processing and are not meant to show the \"best\" way to process bauxite, realistically you will probably want to do chembath and/or bauxite slurry):\n- Macerate -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Grossular byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Thermal Centrifuge -> Macerate (Rutile + Gallium byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Simple/Normal Wash -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Rutile + Grossular (if normal wash) byproduct)\nWhile for most bases, Rutile + Gallium Route 2 is superior, perhaps you already have automated gallium from essence berries. Then you might decide to do Route 3 instead, as it gives 2x more Rutile and therefore 2x more Titanium.\nEvery ore in the game will have a different \"optimal\" route for your run and base needs. This may also change over time. As a result, you want ore processing systems to be flexible - allowing different ores to go on different routes.\nYou also want to be able to automatically pull out things at any step of the process. For example, Rare Earths from GT++ require much higher power load, which would cause your existing macerator line to choke without modifications. You'll want to filter these out and process them in specially dedicated macerators."
- candidate chunk-899f089c10d9b05623dd29d55ffafa96 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > EV Applied Energistics 2] text="Lanthanide processing. This processing line takes in crushed Bastnasite or crushed Monazite to yield the different lanthanide elements.\nFor our purposes, we will only need 6 specific AE components in order to create a rudimentary, and yet infinitely expandable AE ore processing system:\n- ME Controller. This is basic and fundamental. You will need at least 1 to be the brains of your ore processing subnet. Each exposed face can connect to and control up to 32 other AE devices, giving our 1-block controller control over a maximum of 192 devices.\n- ME Drive. This will serve as an overflow buffer, just in case all your machines suffer maintenance issues and shutdown simultaneously.\n- ME Storage Cell. Place inside the ME Drive. You will need at least 1.\n- ME Interfaces (the full block type). These will serve as the import blocks, from which the outputs of your machines will be pushed into to enter the AE network. How many you need depends on how creative you are with your GT piping.\n- ME Storage Buses. You will need 1 for each input bus of your multiblock machines, and one extra to handle overflow (optional).\n- ME OreDictionary Filter Cards. This is the magic that makes AE amazing for ore processing. You will need at least 1 per storage bus, with some exceptions.\nNow, let's talk more about the ME OreDictionary Filter Card, or OreDict cards for short. Many GT items have an OreDictionary Name, that looks something like (item type)(name) . There are OreDict names for the ore block, each of the intermediates during the ore processing steps, and the final pure dust. Using galena as an example, they are:\n- Galena Ore. oreGalena\n- Raw Galena Ore. rawOreGalena\n- Crushed Galena Ore. crushedGalena\n- Purified Galena Ore. crushedPurifiedGalena\n- Centrifuged Galena Ore. crushedCentrifugedGalena\n- Impure Pile of Galena Dust dustImpureGalena\n- Purified Pile of Galena Dust dustPureGalena\n- Galena Dust dustGalena\nME Storage Buses attempt to push out compatible items, when immediately received by the network via either ME Interfaces or ME Import Buses, into the inventories that they face. Conventionally, these would be storage blocks like chests/drawers/barrels. But note that Input Buses also have internal inventories. Thus, when we place an OreDict card inside a ME Storage Bus facing towards an Input Bus, we have created an intelligent exporter of items!\nAnother thing to note is that"
- candidate chunk-8f38ce3e38a37b3091fc7e45fcea268c doc=doc-d38e0fb2c49edead8e172a0081f57d61 sections=[Extreme Voltage (EV) > Progression > Side Quests] text="Side Quests\n-Eliminate microcrafting and improve automation and QoL with AE2 .\n-Build advanced processing lines for Ilmenite and Bauxite.\n-Make a Multiblock Miner for higher ore output.\n-Make various GT++ Multiblock versions of singleblocks such as the Bending Machine, Centrifuge, Lathe and Maceration Stack."

But obviously this isn't very useful.\nSo you start adding \" modules \" to your inbetween conduits. These provide routes for ores to go. Let's start with a macerator module."

### Lane 1 — memory=false

Answer status: answer
Refusal: false
Answer: With gallium already automated from essence berries, the evidence supports prioritizing the route that gives 2x more Rutile: Route 3, Macerate -> Simple/Normal Wash -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge, which yields Rutile + Grossular (if normal wash) byproduct. The evidence states that while Rutile + Gallium Route 2 is superior for most bases, if you already have automated gallium from essence berries, you might decide to do Route 3 instead, as it gives 2x more Rutile and therefore 2x more Titanium.

- candidate chunk-47d289c4d1a14c32f00981954a28a1d8 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > How To Do "Good" Ore Processing? > Flexible] text="Flexible\nOres in GT: New Horizons have multiple \" routes \" you can take which give different outputs. (See the bauxite chart to the right.) For bauxite you can take three routes (Note that these choices simply outline the basic bauxite processing and are not meant to show the \"best\" way to process bauxite, realistically you will probably want to do chembath and/or bauxite slurry):\n- Macerate -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Grossular byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Thermal Centrifuge -> Macerate (Rutile + Gallium byproduct)\n- Macerate -> Simple/Normal Wash -> Macerate -> Simple Wash/Centrifuge (Rutile + Grossular (if normal wash) byproduct)\nWhile for most bases, Rutile + Gallium Route 2 is superior, perhaps you already have automated gallium from essence berries. Then you might decide to do Route 3 instead, as it gives 2x more Rutile and therefore 2x more Titanium.\nEvery ore in the game will have a different \"optimal\" route for your run and base needs. This may also change over time. As a result, you want ore processing systems to be flexible - allowing different ores to go on different routes.\nYou also want to be able to automatically pull out things at any step of the process. For example, Rare Earths from GT++ require much higher power load, which would cause your existing macerator line to choke without modifications. You'll want to filter these out and process them in specially dedicated macerators."
- candidate chunk-f9a7893bcc78ce6dce97919745883b55 doc=doc-8666c624815c422fd88b0cdfb5cbfb2a sections=[High Voltage (HV) > Progression > End Of HV] text="End Of HV\nAfter collection of ores from the Moon, check NEI on how to make Titanium (you process everything into Rutile then react it with Carbon Dust and Chlorine). You will need a stack for turning in the titanium quest to enter the EV tier in the questbook. Note that you must cool Titanium using a Vacuum Freezer after smelting in the EBF.\nSpending some time now looking for a Moon dungeon is an excellent idea too. The mobs are not tough if you've been keeping up at all with your armor, weapons, and health bar. In addition, you are guaranteed to find a T2 rocket schematic in the loot chest (which you absolutely need to progress out of early EV in a standard run), and the quest book will grant you access to your own personal dimension!\nInfrastructure for processing Rutile into Titanium, and Bauxite specifically is recommended, as they are relatively simple multi-step processes and their reactants (not all of Bauxite's, though) can be recycled very simply. Remember, you will be consuming dozens of stacks of Titanium in EV!"
- candidate chunk-899f089c10d9b05623dd29d55ffafa96 doc=doc-10abece1affc7c7ad6fb4e0605bf710b sections=[Ore Processing Concepts > EV Applied Energistics 2] text="Lanthanide processing. This processing line takes in crushed Bastnasite or crushed Monazite to yield the different lanthanide elements.\nFor our purposes, we will only need 6 specific AE components in order to create a rudimentary, and yet infinitely expandable AE ore processing system:\n- ME Controller. This is basic and fundamental. You will need at least 1 to be the brains of your ore processing subnet. Each exposed face can connect to and control up to 32 other AE devices, giving our 1-block controller control over a maximum of 192 devices.\n- ME Drive. This will serve as an overflow buffer, just in case all your machines suffer maintenance issues and shutdown simultaneously.\n- ME Storage Cell. Place inside the ME Drive. You will need at least 1.\n- ME Interfaces (the full block type). These will serve as the import blocks, from which the outputs of your machines will be pushed into to enter the AE network. How many you need depends on how creative you are with your GT piping.\n- ME Storage Buses. You will need 1 for each input bus of your multiblock machines, and one extra to handle overflow (optional).\n- ME OreDictionary Filter Cards. This is the magic that makes AE amazing for ore processing. You will need at least 1 per storage bus, with some exceptions.\nNow, let's talk more about the ME OreDictionary Filter Card, or OreDict cards for short. Many GT items have an OreDictionary Name, that looks something like (item type)(name) . There are OreDict names for the ore block, each of the intermediates during the ore processing steps, and the final pure dust. Using galena as an example, they are:\n- Galena Ore. oreGalena\n- Raw Galena Ore. rawOreGalena\n- Crushed Galena Ore. crushedGalena\n- Purified Galena Ore. crushedPurifiedGalena\n- Centrifuged Galena Ore. crushedCentrifugedGalena\n- Impure Pile of Galena Dust dustImpureGalena\n- Purified Pile of Galena Dust dustPureGalena\n- Galena Dust dustGalena\nME Storage Buses attempt to push out compatible items, when immediately received by the network via either ME Interfaces or ME Import Buses, into the inventories that they face. Conventionally, these would be storage blocks like chests/drawers/barrels. But note that Input Buses also have internal inventories. Thus, when we place an OreDict card inside a ME Storage Bus facing towards an Input Bus, we have created an intelligent exporter of items!\nAnother thing to note is that"
- candidate chunk-48ef13f5915ef08f42de9de271e7ff30 doc=doc-d8847ae5c01144e82bc225fe54375054 sections=[Zero Point Module (ZPM) > Infrastructure Improvements > Neutronium] text="Neutronium\nNeutronium is a new material that is needed in very large quantities in UV and UHV for components and other machines, and it is extremely slow to smelt, taking over two minutes at UV even with the best possible noble gas, and closer to three or four with worse ones. Because of how slow it is and how much it is used, most players passive it, setting up either a Mega Electric Blast Furnace (MEBF) or a large Volcanus array dedicated to passively smelting Neutronium Ingots. The MEBF can be scaled more easily and is much simpler to set up, but due to the high voltage and heat requirements for the Neutronium recipe and the 120% speed bonus of the Volcanus, the latter is more efficient. It is also possible to smelt Neutronium entirely on demand, but doing so requires extremely robust smelting and freezing capacity, or it will be a massive bottleneck."
